(a)

The zigzag paths
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
and the morphism
X_{a_{2}}:L_{1}\to L_{2}

For symmetry reasons, it suffices to prove
\ell_{q}a-a\ell_{q}\in\overline{(\partial_{a}W_{q})}
for a single arc
a\in\check{Q}_{1}
, which we choose to be the arc
a=a_{2}
. Recall that
t(a_{2})=L_{1}
and
h(a_{2})=L_{2}
. The arrow set
\check{Q}_{1}
identifies with odd transversal intersections of zigzag paths in
Q
, and we write
X_{a}
for the odd basis morphism whose corresponding intersection point is located at the midpoint of the arc
a
. For instance, the two zigzag paths
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
and the morphism
X_{a_{2}}:L_{1}\to L_{2}
are depicted in
8(a)
. Recall that the element
b
is a formal sum of the odd transversal intersections
X_{a}
, weighted by their formal parameter
a\in\check{Q}_{1}
. In our case, we have explicitly

b=\sum_{i=1}^{4}a_{i}X_{a_{i}}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}b_{i}X_{b_{i}}.

The first step to centrality is to obtain a candidate expression for
x=\ell_{q,2}a_{2}-a_{2}\ell_{q,1}
in terms of the relations
\partial_{a}W_{q}
. The idea is to inspect those
A_{\infty}
-relations which lead to centrality in the proof of
Lemma 7.30
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(\mu^{\geq 0}(b,\ldots,b))+\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(b,\mu^{\geq 0}_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(b,\ldots,b))\\
+&\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(\mu^{\geq 0}(b,\ldots,b),b)+\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}^{\geq 3}(b,\ldots,\mu^{\geq 0}(b,\ldots,b),\ldots,b)=0.\end{split}

(A.1)

The expression on the left-hand side of (
A.1
) is a sum of multiple odd morphisms weighted by paths in
\check{Q}
. To guess
x
, we extract the coefficient of the odd morphism
X_{a_{2}}
.

Let us inspect all four terms of (
A.1
). The first term vanishes in the case of the 4-punctured torus, since it is geometrically consistent. For the second and third term, recall that
\mu^{\geq 0}(b,\ldots,b)
is the sum of the idententities of the zigzag paths
L_{i}
weighted by
\ell_{q,i}
, and the even intersections
Y_{a}
weighted by the relations
\partial_{a}W_{q}
:

\mu^{\geq 0}(b,\ldots,b)=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\ell_{q,i}\mathrm{id}_{L_{i}}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{a_{i}}W_{q}Y_{a_{i}}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{b_{i}}W_{q}Y_{b_{i}}.

We insert this expression into the second and third terms of (
A.1
) and extract the coefficient of
X_{a_{2}}
. Since there are no 3-gons among zigzag curves in
Q
, the
X_{a_{2}}
coefficient of the second term is
a_{2}\ell_{q,1}
and the
X_{a_{2}}
coefficient of the third term is
-\ell_{q,2}a_{2}
.

For the fourth term in (
A.1
), we have to count CR, ID, DS and DW disks with output
X_{a_{2}}
and input sequence consisting of odd elements of the form
X_{a_{i}}
or
X_{b_{i}}
, mixed with one single even input of the form
Y_{a_{i}}
or
Y_{b_{i}}
. The
X_{a_{2}}
coefficient from every CR, ID, DS or DW disk is then the path in
\check{Q}
given by the concatenation of the
a_{i}
or
b_{i}
symbols, inserting the relation
\partial_{a_{i}}W_{q}
or
\partial_{b_{i}}W_{q}
instead at the even input.

It remains to enumerate all relevant CR, ID, DS and DW disks explicitly in the case of the 4-punctured torus:

•

CR disks are 4-gons, in fact rectangles due to the shape of
Q
. CR disks contribute both to the inner and to the outer
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}^{3}
in (
A.1
).

•

ID disks are 5-gons, in fact rectangles of which one side has an output marking on the identity location
a_{2}
. They are only relevant for the inner
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
since their output is an identity and not
X_{a_{2}}
.

•

DS disks are only relevant for the outer
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
since they require at least one even input. According to the intricate rules for DS disks, the only DS disk with output
X_{a_{2}}
is the DS disk with infinitesimally small side lengths contributing to
\mu^{3}_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(X_{a_{2}},Y_{a_{2}},X_{a_{2}})
. The existence of this DS disk is independent of the choice of co-identity location. In terms of the Kadeishvili construction from
[
24
]
, this DS disk corresponds to the
\pi
-tree with associated DS result component depicted in
Figure A.9
.

•

DW disks are irrelevant. In fact, a DW disk has at least one even input and has a co-identity output and can therefore neither contribute to the inner
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
nor contribute
X_{a_{2}}
to the outer
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
.

This finishes our evaluation of (
A.1
). All in all, the
A_{\infty}
-relations for
\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}
claim that the
X_{a_{2}}
coefficients of (
A.1
) add up to zero. This provides us with a candidate expression for
x=\ell_{q,2}a_{2}-a_{2}\ell_{q,1}
in terms of the relations
\partial_{a}W_{q}
. We record and verify this guess as follows:

\alpha_{4}
\mathrm{id}
(C)
\alpha_{4}
\mu^{2}=\alpha_{4}
h_{q}=\mathrm{id}_{b_{1}}
\mu^{2}=\alpha_{4}
\pi_{q}=\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}

Figure A.9
:
DS result component contributing to
\mu^{3}(X_{a_{2}},Y_{a_{2}},X_{a_{2}})

Lemma A.1
.

Regard the 4-punctured torus
Q
with its zigzag paths
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3},L_{4}
. Denote by
\ell_{q,1}
and
\ell_{q,2}
the potentials in
\mathbb{C}\check{Q}\llbracket q_{1},q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}\rrbracket
associated with
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
under the choice of
a_{2}
as identity location. Then within
\mathbb{C}\check{Q}\llbracket q_{1},q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}\rrbracket
we have

\displaystyle\ell_{q,2}a_{2}-a_{2}\ell_{q,1}=

\displaystyle~a_{2}(\partial_{a_{2}}W_{q})a_{2}

(A.2)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q^{l}q_{14}^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{23}q^{l}(q_{23}q)^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{3}b_{4}(\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}q^{2l}q_{14}^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{23}qq^{2l}(q_{23}q)^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{2}b_{1}(\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{2}q_{4}q^{l}(q_{14}q)^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{2}q^{l}q_{23}^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{3}b_{1}(\partial_{b_{4}}W_{q})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}qq^{2l}(q_{14}q)^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{23}q^{2l}q_{23}^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{2}b_{4}(\partial_{b_{4}}W_{q})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{2}q^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{2}q^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{3}(\partial_{a_{2}}W_{q})a_{3}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q^{2}q^{2l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q^{2}q^{2l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{2}(\partial_{a_{2}}W_{q})a_{2}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}qq^{l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}qq^{l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{3}(\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q})a_{2}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}qq^{2l}q^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}qq^{2l}q^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)a_{2}(\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q})a_{3}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{2}q_{14}(q_{14}q)^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{2}q_{23}^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)(\partial_{b_{2}}W_{q})b_{3}a_{3}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}q(q_{14}q)^{l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{23}q_{23}^{l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}\bigg)(\partial_{b_{2}}W_{q})b_{2}a_{2}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{14}^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{23}(q_{23}q)^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}\bigg)(\partial_{b_{3}}W_{q})b_{2}a_{3}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}q_{14}^{l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{23}q(q_{23}q)^{l}q^{2k}q^{2kl}\bigg)(\partial_{b_{3}}W_{q})b_{3}a_{2}.

In particular, we have
\ell_{q}a_{2}=a_{2}\ell_{q}
within
\operatorname{Jac}_{q}\check{Q}
.

Proof.

The strategy is to cancel terms on the right-hand side against each other. The correct means of finding cancellation partners is by tracing back all terms to their respective geometric origins. More precisely, recall that the claimed identity (
A.2
) is supposed to reflect the
A_{\infty}
-relations for
\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}
. The
A_{\infty}
-relations again reflect the geometric property that nonconvex disks can be divided into convex disks in two different ways. Once we trace back every term on the right-hand side of (
A.2
) to its nonconvex disk origin, we find the correct cancellation partner. A few corner cases remain in which the disk is actually convex, and these correspond to the left-hand side.

It might be tempting to cancel the eight middle terms on the right-hand side of (
A.2
) first. However, the cancellation of these eight terms is specific to the case of the 4-punctured torus and we in fact need to preserve these terms in order to make the other cancellations transparent.

To get started, note that the identity (
A.2
) consists of
\mathbb{C}\llbracket q_{1},q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}\rrbracket
-linear combinations of paths of length five. Checking the identity entails comparing coefficients of every of the possible paths. In the remainder of the proof, we merely focus on two example paths:
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{3}a_{2}
and
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{2}a_{3}
. Out of these two, the case of the first path is easily settled because the right-hand side contains no such paths. The case of the second path is much harder and truly illustrates the geometric reason for equality. The authors also checked manually the coefficients of all 14 other paths and used the results to eradicate all errors in (
A.2
). For the present demonstration, we merely restrict to the two paths mentioned.

Let us comment on the first path
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{3}a_{2}
. We determine the coefficient of this path in all four entities of (
A.2
). The coefficient coming from the term
\ell_{q,2}a_{2}
is

\sum_{k,l\geq 0}(k+1)q_{4}(q_{23}q)^{l}q_{14}^{k}q^{2kl}+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}kq_{4}q_{14}^{l}(q_{23}q)^{k}q^{2kl}.

The coefficient coming from the term
a_{2}\ell_{q,1}
is

\sum_{k,l\geq 0}lq_{4}(q_{23}q)^{l}q_{14}^{k}q^{2kl}+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}(l+1)q_{4}q_{14}^{l}(q_{23}q)^{k}q^{2kl}.

The coefficient coming from the term
a_{2}(\partial_{a_{2}}W_{q})a_{2}
is

\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{4}q_{14}^{k}(q_{23}q)^{l}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{4}q_{14}^{l}(q_{23}q)^{k}q^{2kl}=0.

Note that this vanishing is specific to the 4-punctured torus. The coefficient in the rest of the right-hand side of (
A.2
) vanishes because
\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q}
has vanishing
a_{1}b_{3}a_{2}
coefficient, because
\partial_{a_{2}}W_{q}
has vanishing
b_{1}a_{1}b_{3}
coefficient, and because
\partial_{b_{3}}W_{q}
has vanishing
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}
coefficient. All in all, both the left-hand side and the right-hand side of (
A.2
) cancel out. This proves the identity (
A.2
) on the level of
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{3}a_{2}
terms.

We now focus on the second path
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{2}a_{3}\in\mathbb{C}\check{Q}
and prove that its coefficient in (
A.2
) vanishes. Regarding the left-hand side, the coefficient in
\ell_{q,2}a_{2}
vanishes and the coefficient in
-a_{2}\ell_{q,1}
consists of the two sums

-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}(l+1)q(q_{23}q)^{l}(q_{14}q)^{k}q^{2kl}-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}lq_{14}^{l}q_{23}^{k}q^{2kl}.

(A.3)

Regarding the right-hand side, the coefficient in
a_{2}(\partial_{a_{2}}W_{q})a_{2}
vanishes. The relevant contribution of the 24 remaining relation terms is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{4}q^{2l}q_{14}^{k}q^{2kl}a_{2}b_{1}(\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q})\\
&-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{2}q_{3}qq^{2l}(q_{23}q)^{k}q^{2kl}a_{2}b_{1}(\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q})\\
&-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}qq^{2l}q^{k}q^{2kl}a_{2}(\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q})a_{3}\\
&+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}qq^{2l}q^{k}q^{2kl}a_{2}(\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q})a_{3}\\
&+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{14}^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}(\partial_{b_{3}}W_{q})b_{2}a_{3}\\
&-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}(q_{23}q)^{l}q^{k}q^{2kl}(\partial_{b_{3}}W_{q})b_{2}a_{3}\end{split}

(A.4)

We count the
a_{1}b_{2}a_{3}
coefficients in
\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q}
,
\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q}
and
\partial_{b_{3}}W_{q}
as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q}|_{a_{1}b_{2}a_{3}}=

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}^{2k+1+l+2kl}q_{23}^{2l+1+k+2kl}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}^{l+2kl}q_{23}^{k+2kl}

\displaystyle\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q}|_{b_{1}a_{1}b_{2}}=

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}^{2k+l+1+2kl}q_{23}^{2l+k+1+2kl}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{14}^{l+2kl}q_{23}^{k+2kl}

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{3}}|a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}=

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{4}q_{14}^{k+l+2kl}q_{23}^{2l+2kl}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k,l\geq 0}q_{4}q_{14}^{k+l+2kl}q_{23}^{2k+2kl}

Plugging these expressions into (
A.4
), we get twelve terms which we name as follows:

1A:

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)}q_{23}^{2(k+1)l+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)}

1B:

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+l^{\prime}(2k^{\prime}+1)}q_{23}^{2(k+1)l+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)}

2A:

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)}q_{23}^{2(k+1)(l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)}

2B:

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}}q_{23}^{2(k+1)(l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)}

3A:

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)}

3B:

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)}

4A:

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)}

4B:

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)}

5A:

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(1+l+k+2kl)+(k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}l^{\prime})}q_{23}^{k(2l+1)+2l^{\prime}(k^{\prime}+1)}

5B:

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(1+l+k+2kl)+(k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}l^{\prime})}q_{23}^{k(2l+1)+2k^{\prime}(l^{\prime}+1)}

6A:

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(1+l+k+2kl)+(k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}l^{\prime})}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+2(k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}}

6B:

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{(1+l+k+2kl)+(k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}l^{\prime})}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2l+1)+2k^{\prime}(l^{\prime}+1)}

It is our task to sum up these twelve terms (1A–6B) and prove the sum equal to (
A.3
). Again, note that 3A and 3B cancel with 4A and 4B. However, performing this easy cancellation would obfuscate all other cancellations. Instead, we shall obtain the correct cancellations by inspecting the geometric origin of every of these twelve terms.

We give a catalog of all cancellations below. To understand the reasons behind the cancellations, we shall demonstrate here how one finds the correct cancellation for the term 1A in case
l\geq l^{\prime}+1
. Its geometric origin is depicted in
10(a)
. In short, this figure depicts a pair of disks contributing to

\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(X_{a_{2}},X_{b_{1}},\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(X_{a_{1}},X_{b_{2}},X_{a_{3}})).

(A.5)

More precisely, the disk labeled
\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q}
contributes to the inner
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
, while the disk labeled “coeff” contributes to the outer
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
. The side lengths of the disk labeled
\partial_{b_{1}}W_{q}
are
4k^{\prime}+3
and
4l^{\prime}+3
. Correspondingly, the example depicted has
k^{\prime}=l^{\prime}=0
. The side lengths of the disk labeled “coeff” are
4k+4
and
4l+1
. Correspondingly, the example depicted has
k=0
and
l=1
. The nonconvex shape can be split into two other pieces, depicted in
10(b)
. That figure depicts a pair of disks contributing to

\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(X_{a_{2}},\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}(X_{b_{1}},X_{a_{1}},X_{b_{2}}),X_{a_{3}}).

(A.6)

The two individual contributions to (
A.5
) and (
A.6
) cover the same area and therefore have the same deformation parameters. A quick check shows that they have equal sign. Noting that 1A and 3A come with opposite overall signs, this makes 3A our candidate for cancellation with 1A in case
l\geq l^{\prime}+1
.

The cancellation of 1A and 3A involves a change of indices. More precisely, the term 1A with indices
(k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
cancels with 3A with indices
(k,l-l^{\prime}-1,k+k^{\prime}+1,l^{\prime})
. To obtain this correspondence between indices, let us understand the disks in
10(b)
. The disk labeled
\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q}
contributes to the inner
\mu_{\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}}
, while the disk labeled “coeff” contributes to the outer
\operatorname{H}\mathbb{L}_{q}
. The side lengths of the disk labeled
\partial_{a_{3}}W_{q}
are
4(k+k^{\prime}+1)+3
and
4l^{\prime}+3
. The side lengths of the disk labeled “coeff” are
4k+4
and
4(l-l^{\prime}-1)+2
. This means we expect to find the partner of 1A with indices
(k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime})
in 3A with indices
(k,l-l^{\prime}-1,k+k^{\prime}+1,l^{\prime})
.

Similar considerations for all other pairs of terms give rise to a list of cancellation candidates. In what follows, we compile this list and check for every entry individually that it indeed cancels out with its partner:

•

1A in case
l\geq l^{\prime}+1
cancels with 3A with indices
(k,l-l^{\prime}-1,k+k^{\prime}+1,l^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l-l^{\prime}-1)+1)+(2(k+k^{\prime}+1)+1)(l^{\prime}+1),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)l+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l-l^{\prime}-1)+1)+((k+k^{\prime}+1)+1)(2l^{\prime}+1).

•

1A in case
l\leq l^{\prime}
cancels with 6A with indices
(k^{\prime},l^{\prime}-l,k+k^{\prime}+1,l)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(1+(l^{\prime}-l)+k^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}(l^{\prime}-l))+((k+k^{\prime}+1)+l+2(k+k^{\prime}+1)l),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)l+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k^{\prime}+1)(2(l^{\prime}-l)+1)+2((k+k^{\prime}+1)+1)l.

•

1B in case
k\geq k^{\prime}
cancels with 5A with indices
(k^{\prime},l+l^{\prime},k-k^{\prime},l)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+l^{\prime}(2k^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(1+(l+l^{\prime})+k^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}(l+l^{\prime}))+((k-k^{\prime})+l+2(k-k^{\prime})l),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)l+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=k^{\prime}(2(l+l^{\prime})+1)+2l((k-k^{\prime})+1).

•

1B in case
k+1\leq k^{\prime}
cancels with 3B with indices
(k,l+l^{\prime},k^{\prime}-k-1,l^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+l^{\prime}(2k^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l+l^{\prime})+1)+(2(k^{\prime}-k-1)+1)l^{\prime},

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)l+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l+l^{\prime})+1)+(k^{\prime}-k-1)(2l^{\prime}+1).

•

2A in case
k+1\leq k^{\prime}
cancels with 4A with indices
(k,l+l^{\prime}+1,k^{\prime}-k-1,l^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l+l^{\prime}+1)+1)+(2(k^{\prime}-k-1)+1)(l^{\prime}+1),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)(l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l+l^{\prime}+1)+1)+((k^{\prime}-k-1)+1)(2l^{\prime}+1).

•

2A in case
k\geq k^{\prime}
cancels with 6B with indices
(k^{\prime},l+l^{\prime}+1,k-k^{\prime},l)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(1+(l+l^{\prime}+1)+k^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}(l+l^{\prime}+1))+((k-k^{\prime})+l+2(k-k^{\prime})l)

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)(l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k^{\prime}+1)(2(l+l^{\prime}+1)+1)+2(k-k^{\prime})(l+1).

•

2B in case
l\geq l^{\prime}
cancels with 4B with indices
(k,l-l^{\prime},k+k^{\prime}+1,l^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l-l^{\prime})+1)+(2(k+k^{\prime}+1)+1)l^{\prime}.

\displaystyle(q_{23}):2(k+1)(l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+1)(2(l-l^{\prime})+1)+(k+k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1).

•

2B in case
l+1\leq l^{\prime}
cancels with 5B with indices
(k^{\prime},l^{\prime}-l-1,k+k^{\prime}+1,l)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(1+(l^{\prime}-l-1)+k^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}(l^{\prime}-l-1))+((k+k^{\prime}+1)+l+2(k+k^{\prime}+1)l),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle 2(k+1)(l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=k^{\prime}(2(l^{\prime}-l-1)+1)+2(k+k^{\prime}+1)(l+1).

•

3A in case
k^{\prime}\leq k
cancels with 5A with indices
(k-k^{\prime},l,k^{\prime},l+l^{\prime}+1)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(1+l+(k-k^{\prime})+2(k-k^{\prime})l)+(k^{\prime}+(l+l^{\prime}+1)+2k^{\prime}(l+l^{\prime}+1)),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k-k^{\prime})(2l+1)+2(l+l^{\prime}+1)(k^{\prime}+1).

•

3B in case
l^{\prime}\geq l+1
cancels with 5B with indices
(k+k^{\prime}+1,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}-l-1)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(1+l+(k+k^{\prime}+1)+2(k+k^{\prime}+1)l)+(k^{\prime}+(l^{\prime}-l-1)+2k^{\prime}(l^{\prime}-l-1)),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(k+k^{\prime}+1)(2l+1)+2k^{\prime}((l^{\prime}-l-1)+1).

•

4A in case
l^{\prime}\geq l
cancels with 6A with indices
(k+k^{\prime}+1,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}-l)
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=(1+l+(k+k^{\prime}+1)+2(k+k^{\prime}+1)l)+(k^{\prime}+(l^{\prime}-l)+2k^{\prime}(l^{\prime}-l)),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(k^{\prime}+1)(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=((k+k^{\prime}+1)+1)(2l+1)+2(k^{\prime}+1)(l^{\prime}-l).

•

4B in case
k\geq k^{\prime}
cancels with 6B with indices
(k-k^{\prime},l,k^{\prime},l+l^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle(q_{14}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+(2k^{\prime}+1)l^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(1+l+(k-k^{\prime})+2(k-k^{\prime})l)+(k^{\prime}+(l+l^{\prime})+2k^{\prime}(l+l^{\prime})),

\displaystyle(q_{23}):

\displaystyle(k+1)(2l+1)+k^{\prime}(2l^{\prime}+1)

\displaystyle=((k-k^{\prime})+1)(2l+1)+2k^{\prime}((l+l^{\prime})+1).

We have shown that most terms of (
A.4
) cancel out. It remains to analyze the corner cases which did not cancel and match them with (
A.3
). In fact, the only remaining corner terms are 6A with indices
(k,l,k,l^{\prime})
and 5B with indices
(k,l,k,l^{\prime})
. We conclude that the sum of the 12 terms (1A–6B) is

\displaystyle-\sum_{k,l,l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{1+(l+l^{\prime})+2k+2k(l+l^{\prime})}q_{23}^{(k+1)(2(l+l^{\prime})+1)}-\sum_{k,l,l^{\prime}\geq 0}q_{14}^{1+(l+l^{\prime})+2k+2k(l+l^{\prime})}q_{23}^{k(2(l+l^{\prime})+3)}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k,s\geq 0}(s+1)q_{14}^{1+s+2k+2ks}q_{23}^{1+k+2s+2ks}-\sum_{k,s\geq 0}sq_{14}^{s+2ks}q_{23}^{k+2ks}.

We recognize this expression as equal to (
A.3
). In other words, the coefficient of
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{2}a_{3}
in the centrality identity (
A.2
) agrees on both sides. This finishes the checks for the coefficients of
a_{2}b_{1}a_{1}b_{2}a_{3}
, and thereby finishes our selected calculations aimed at demonstrating (
A.2
).
∎
